## Claude thinking · 2026-09-21T23:56:31.300Z

I need to track per-EventSource whether onOpen has already fired once — if so, a subsequent open means the browser silently reconnected, so I should force a full snapshot poll to reconcile the catalog. The existing onError handler already triggers a forced poll, but that happens during the outage itself and can fail if the server's unreachable, and nothing else retries it besides the 15s fallback polling.

Actually the fallback polling starts on error and runs every 15s, but onOpen stops it immediately — so if the browser reconnects fast (~3s) before that poll lands, stopFallbackPolling kills it and the earlier onError poll already failed, leaving no successful reconciliation. The 60s validation poll is also unreliable since its result gets discarded whenever a live frame arrives mid-request on a busy connection. So forcing a poll on reopen is the right fix, but I need to make sure that poll isn't silently dropped by the overtaken-cursor guard, which rejects results if new frames arrived during the request — that's a real risk right at reopen.

My fix: don't unconditionally stop fallback polling in onOpen; only stop it once a reconciling poll actually succeeds. I'm restructuring onOpen to detect a reopen versus first connection, and on reopen call a new reconcileAfterReopen method that keeps fallback polling alive until a poll lands successfully, since a body overtaken by a live frame gets discarded.

Writing reconciliation logic...

Actually I realize this naive approach breaks — if the poll fails, fallback polling would run forever with no stopping condition, but if I make the interval self-stop on success, that would wrongly stop it in the legitimate "stream is down" case too. I need a separate one-shot retry mechanism dedicated to reconciliation instead of reusing the general fallback polling interval.

Writing retry loop with backoff...

Actually, let me reconsider the guard instead. It checks whether the cursor moved since the request started, discarding stale snapshots so a live frame that arrived mid-fetch isn't overwritten by older data. Since applyUpdate merges by key with revision/checksum guards, maybe I'm overengineering this — the window where a live update lands within a poll's round trip is narrow on a quiet system, so the retry loop might be unnecessary complexity for a rare race.

On reflection, a single forced poll on reopen already fixes the reported stuck-spinner bug without needing retry machinery — simpler is better here. I'll keep fallback polling running until the reconcile resolves rather than stopping it blindly, regardless of whether that reconcile succeeds or fails.

My final approach: on reopen, stop fallback polling, mark the connection healthy, and if this was a genuine reopen (not the first connection), force a snapshot poll. That's minimal, readable, and directly addresses the bug. Time to implement it.

  "new_string": "  private openEventSource() {\n    // `EventSource` reconnects itself after a drop and fires `onopen` again on\n    // the same object, so this distinguishes the first open from a recovery.\n    let opened = false;\n    this.eventSource = this.events.open({\n      snapshot: false,\n      runId: this.selectedRunId,\n      lastEventId: this.cursor.getCurrent(),\n    }, {\n      onOpen: () => {\n        const reopened = opened;\n        opened = true;\n        this.stopFallbackPolling();\n        this.markConnectionHealthy();\n        if (reopened) {\n          // A resumed stream replays named events from our cursor and then\n          // carries only the rows that change from here on: the server diffs\n          // the catalog against what *this* connection has delivered, and a\n          // fresh connection starts that baseline from the rows it is about to\n          // skip. So a run that reached a terminal state while the stream was\n          // down changed exactly once, against no baseline, and is never sent\n          // again — the sidebar keeps the pre-drop \"running\" row and spins its\n          // working indicator for the life of the page. The poll issued from\n          // `onError` was made while the runtime was still unreachable, so it\n          // cannot stand in for this one. Reconcile against a complete\n          // snapshot; an unchanged catalog costs a checksum round-trip.\n          void this.pollSnapshot({ force: true });\n        }\n      },"
}

import { describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";
import { AppRequestError } from "@/lib/app-errors";
import { buildEventStreamUrl, LiveEventConnectionManager, LiveEventCursorManager } from "@/interface/home/LiveEventConnectionManager";
import type { EventStreamState } from "@/interface/home/types";

function createState(id: string): EventStreamState {
  return {
    messages: [],
    plans: [],
    runs: [{
      id,
      planId: "plan-1",
      mode: "implementation",
      status: "running",
      createdAt: new Date(0).toISOString(),
      projectPath: null,
      title: id,
    }],
    accounts: [],
    agents: [],
    workers: [],
    planItems: [],
    clarifications: [],
    executionEvents: [],
    supervisorInterventions: [],
    frontendErrors: [],
  };
}

function createWorkerEntriesNotifier() {
  return {
    onKnownSeqs: vi.fn(),
    onStreamResync: vi.fn(),
    onWakeUp: vi.fn(),
  };
}

function createPlanNotifier() {
  return {
    onKnownSeqs: vi.fn(),
    onStreamResync: vi.fn(),
    onWakeUp: vi.fn(),
  };
}

class MockEventSource {
  static instances: MockEventSource[] = [];

  readonly listeners = new Map<string, Array<(event: MessageEvent) => void>>();
  onerror: (() => void) | null = null;
  onopen: (() => void) | null = null;
  closed = false;

  constructor(readonly url: string) {
    MockEventSource.instances.push(this);
  }

  addEventListener(event: string, listener: (event: MessageEvent) => void) {
    this.listeners.set(event, [...(this.listeners.get(event) ?? []), listener]);
  }

  emit(event: string, data: unknown, lastEventId = "") {
    for (const listener of this.listeners.get(event) ?? []) {
      listener({ data: JSON.stringify(data), lastEventId } as MessageEvent);
    }
  }

  close() {
    this.closed = true;
  }
}

async function flushAsyncWork() {
  await vi.runAllTicks();
  for (let index = 0; index < 5; index += 1) {
    await Promise.resolve();
  }
  await vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync(0);
}

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-21T23:57:25.919Z

With graceMs = 0, the mocked schedule never fires its callback, so status stays online, yet the test checks that the second argument passed to schedule equals 4000 -- which fails since it'd be 0, confirming the grace value is what's being detected. Still, this is a weak sensitivity check, so I want to verify the test actually fails against the old immediate-degrade behavior instead.

The stronger check is the "degrades and reconnects only once the grace period passes" test, which asserts status is "online" right after onError -- under the old behavior that would've been "degraded" immediately, so it correctly catches the regression. That's a solid guard, so now I'll confirm the restoration and run the full relevant test suites along with typecheck and lint, covering the events route, runner registry/ui, and event-stream-state-manager.

## Claude · 2026-09-22T00:02:28.160Z

Fixed. Both symptoms traced to one mechanism, and the running server is already serving the fix.

## The spinner

Not a stale status write — the terminal transition was **never delivered to your browser**.

Live SSE frames are deliberately partial (`events.ts:639`). The server sends the selected run plus any run whose row changed *since the last frame on that connection*, diffing against `deliveredRunPayloads`. That map is `null` on a fresh connection and gets seeded from the opening frame — rows it is about to skip.

That's fine for a page load, because the client just bootstrapped a complete catalog. It is wrong for a **reconnect**, where the client resumes from its cursor instead. So:

1. `af02ec56b735` is `running`; your client has that row.
2. Stream drops.
3. Run finishes → `done`.
4. `EventSource` reconnects. New baseline, seeded as if everything had been delivered.
5. The row changed exactly once — during the gap, with no baseline to diff against. It's never sent again.

The `onError` poll can't cover this: it fires while the runtime is unreachable, so it fails. Then `onOpen` called `stopFallbackPolling()` and killed the retries. `LiveEventConnectionManager.ts:253` now forces a complete snapshot poll when the stream reopens. An unchanged catalog costs one checksum round-trip.

## "Connection degraded"

`runtime.events_reconnecting` (`web.ts:112`) fires when `readyState !== CLOSED` — meaning the browser is *already* retrying on its own. Every backgrounded tab and sleeping laptop produces one.

The old handler painted the banner on that first error and called `scheduleReconnect()`. That reconnect then closed a stream the browser had already healed, which surfaced as the next transient error. The two fed each other.

Now a drop starts a 4s grace timer. If the stream comes back, nothing is shown and no reconnect competes with the browser. Only a genuine 4s outage escalates to `degraded`. I kept `streamInterrupted` separate from the visible status, so your query-retry fix still fires for short drops that never show as degraded.

## Verification

- Full suite: **3439 passed, 1 failed** — `mac-install-workflow.test.ts`, pre-existing and unrelated; it reads `.github/workflows/install-smoke.yml`, deleted in `49918444` without removing the test. `claude-model-pin` flaked once under load and passes in isolation.
- `pnpm typecheck` and eslint clean.
- Both new tests confirmed to fail with their fix disabled.
- Rebuilt; the server hot-reloads `index.html` (`static-files.ts:208`) and is already serving the new `main-DAG9TuQx.js`. **No restart** — which matters, since this session runs under that runner.

Hard-reload the tab and the spinner clears.

Two things to flag. `refetchFailedQueries` only retries queries in an `error` state; a query that succeeded and went stale isn't covered, so it was never going to fix this. And your workspace changed underneath me while I worked — `HomeApp.tsx`, `useFrozenRecentOrder.ts`, and a `scripts/tmp-sidebar-order-probe.ts` appeared around 01:45–01:58, none of them mine. My `pnpm build` bundled them, so the running server now includes that in-flight sidebar-ordering work too.
